A name in the making · September 30, 2026

A moon by any other name.

A person becomes an ocean moon. A clear sphere catches light, water moves, and a little mischief slips through. What should we call it?

Rank a few names ↓
A spherical world, held in light.
Illustrative mark; not a rendering of Enceladus.

The current name is provisional. Thatsnomoon, thatsnozorb and Enceladus Within are working labels. This page exists to explore their replacement; every candidate remains open for Andrew’s decision.

230 entries, 227 distinct names; mesh levels 0–5 available in the editor. 32 fully checked clean; 56 with a collision; 139 checked but incomplete; 0 not checked.

Codex authored the morpheme families before generation, using the project files. The directions below are editorial preferences, separate from namespace availability.

Invent · mesh · rank · remember

Find the name that floats.

20 names invented by Codex, 40 three-word meshes, 40 four-word meshes, and 40 multi-mesh descendants from submitted names. Word count and blend strength are independent. Gentle joins keep the story audible; the stronger experiments show where it begins to dissolve.

Each AI gets up to 15 final picks in total, including earlier choices, drawn from all surviving collections. These shelves keep the models’ final choices separate from your saved favorites. Forgotten names stay hidden. The final review includes the complete brief, roots, explanations, both score sets, weight proposals and calculated rankings. Latest availability evidence appears in these ranked cards and the review download; the historical report below keeps its earlier dated receipts.

Grok’s final 15
Andrew’s creations

Your submissions stay in Andrew’s creations. Each generated name starts from one submitted word, then joins words from the checked families. Choose the word count and blend strength in the palette.

Twelve scores · change what matters

Each name has two independent reviews. Shared categories use the mean of ChatGPT’s and Grok’s scores; their personal preferences stay separate. Scores run from 0 to 10, then the sliders weight the total out of 100 and rerank immediately. Open a name to compare both reviewers, see its weighted contributions, or enter your own score.

The starting preset keeps ChatGPT preference at 50% and adds Grok preference at 10%. Try either model’s suggested weights or their normalized average. A name with missing weighted scores waits for review; it does not receive invented scores. New live rolls keep their original estimates in their details.

What each score means

Cleverness replaces water play; wordplay replaces light and morphing. Original scores and weights stay in your exported shelves. Saved custom weights keep only categories whose meanings are unchanged; use a preset to give the new categories weight. Creative scores never change the research verdict. Known collisions remain in the archive and in Andrew’s creations.

Forgotten / earlier rolls

Forgetting hides a name from the main ranking. It stays here with its scores and research; restore it whenever it catches your ear again. Previous live rolls also stay here. Saved favorites and Andrew’s creations keep their own shelves.

Shelves, score edits and weights persist in this browser, not across devices. Export a portable backup before clearing browser storage.

Checked directions worth saying aloud.

Only names with conclusive negative namespace checks and no recorded near-name matches appear here. There are currently 4 checked editorial directions. Collisions and incomplete results remain in the complete roll below.

  1. fathomoon · FATH-uh-moon

    fathom + moon · clean

    Fathom means both a measure of depth and an act of understanding. Blending its final m with moon joins the ocean within the moon to the audience’s act of looking into it.

    This is the strongest poetic direction in this batch. It leaves room for body, water and cosmic scale without making the zorb apparatus the whole identity; the extra syllable is the price of that richness.

  2. selenerime · seh-LEE-neh-rime

    selene + rime · clean

    Selene brings the moon’s mythic identity and rime brings a surface of frozen water. Both roots stay audible, giving the installation a quiet ice-and-moon character.

    A poetic direction for Enceladus’s bright shell, with room for the hidden ocean story underneath. Selene’s pronunciation may need introducing, and the fun and human movement would come through the artwork rather than the name alone.

  3. causticuddle · KAW-stih-KUD-ul

    caustic + cuddle · clean

    In optics, a caustic is the bright pattern formed when light is concentrated by a curved surface; cuddle supplies warmth and bodily closeness. Sharing c gives the pair a playful seam.

    This is a deliberately odd lensing-and-affection direction. The optical meaning is highly relevant, but the everyday harsh or chemical meaning of caustic is a real naming drawback.

  4. apsisoma · ap-sih-SOH-ma

    apsis + soma · clean

    An apsis is a near or far turning point along an orbit; soma means body. Sharing s gives the name a connection between celestial motion and the performer’s presence.

    This is an expressive body-and-orbit direction with a smooth spoken rhythm. Both roots are less familiar than moon or bubble, so its meaning would benefit from a short accompanying description.

Ice, zorb, ocean.

Andrew's latest directions, kept beside their availability evidence.

  1. cryocean

    cryo + ocean · taken

    Cryo and ocean meet at their shared o, putting the ice shell and the hidden water world into one compact word. It captures Enceladus's central contrast with very little explanation. A possible spoken reading is cry-OH-shun, though the spelling can invite other stresses; the existing-use receipt still applies.

    Andrew's new direction. Existing cryOcean research project and a registered .com are recorded; this name is not available across the checked sources.

  2. zorbit

    zorb + orbit · taken

    Zorb and orbit overlap around orb, so the playful sphere becomes a small world in motion. It is short enough to call across the installation and keeps both the physical object and the space theme audible. The lively sound is a strength, but the recorded existing-use result remains a separate concern.

    Andrew called this a strong direction. Existing exact uses include a moisture-control product, npm package, GitHub repositories and registered domains.

  3. cryzorcean

    cryo + zorb + ocean · unverified

    Cryo supplies ice, zorb supplies the vessel, and ocean supplies the hidden water world. The compressed zor seam places the sphere inside the cold-ocean idea, echoing the installation's transformation. A possible reading is cry-ZOR-shun; it has a strong center, though the three-root meaning and spelling take a little explanation.

    Andrew's exploratory three-root blend. No exact use found in the quoted web search and all five checked domains returned absence, but Bluesky and Reddit checks were unavailable. It remains unverified.

Make it your own

Play with the words.

Check words to include them. Uncheck a family to pause it. × removes a word or family and remembers the choice; refill supplies different words. Your draft stays in this browser. Choose two, three or four input words, then choose blend strength separately. Multi mesh randomizes a level from 0–4 at each join; every result records those levels. Keep at least as many active families as input words. New play words focus on splashing, paddling, puddles and dunking; use the water-play button to add them to an older saved draft.

Add a new family

The words before the names.

The original roll used twelve families of twelve words. The editor now has ten extra authored directions—bioluminescence, floating, ocean life, dream/theatre, life/growth, doubling/cell division, genesis/emergence, protoplasm/early life, space travel and astral projection—and ten reserve words in each family for refills. Every generated name combines roots across family boundaries. These roots are ordinary words, names and reusable word parts; the term “morpheme” follows the generator’s naming vocabulary.

causticuddle

caustic + cuddle

clean

lensing_optics + play_delight

In optics, a caustic is the bright pattern formed when light is concentrated by a curved surface; cuddle supplies warmth and bodily closeness. Sharing c gives the pair a playful seam.

This is a deliberately odd lensing-and-affection direction. The optical meaning is highly relevant, but the everyday harsh or chemical meaning of caustic is a real naming drawback.

Read the marks honestly.

  • clean means every enabled namespace layer answered conclusively. taken means an exact namespace collision or an existing use was found. unverified means checks ran but some sources could not answer. not-checked means no fresh receipt was collected for that entry.
  • The earlier historical audit below used eighteen namespace sources. Current interactive research uses just Brave, GitHub, npm and .com, and never gates exploration. The historical sources are GitHub, seven package registries, five domain extensions (.com, .io, .dev, .ai and .app), and five social services. This audit retried domains through authoritative RDAP and registry WHOIS, validated registered controls, and retained still-fresh conclusive native receipts where later retries were rate-limited. The RDAP relay’s unsupported .io response is never accepted as availability evidence.
  • Exact-name web searches additionally checked the leading directions and found existing uses missed by the namespace grid. Existing-use links appear in each affected candidate’s evidence. A no-hit web search remains limited evidence. Results are dated snapshots; they do not establish trademark clearance or reserve a name.
  • Codex wrote the candidate explanations and 4 checked editorial directions. No Qwen, MiniMax or Grok ranking is claimed for this report. The two-root inputs are preserved even when the affix or phonetic output loses their audible meaning.
  • The specific moon is Enceladus. NASA describes its icy shell, salty ocean and southern plumes. The artwork’s projected interior is artistic interpretation.
  • Project sources: the grant draft, v3 design decisions, optics/capture notes and media-only show controller. This page explores artistic identity; the existing project record describes what has been built.
